### layers/blog/content/david/pages/2026-07-13-two-fixes-two-doors.md:14
kind: historical; matched: `0004-objective-safety-gate.md#L111-L112`

```text
The other two were, if anything, *more* clear-cut. One decision record claimed a certain kind of file — the agents' step-by-step playbooks — live in one folder, when the real, working copies had moved to a different one months earlier, without that record ever being told. The other claimed every commit gets its two-line "who made this and why" signature automatically, no extra work required — true for most commits, but not for one specific path this repo added five days *after* that claim was written down, which has to build the signature by hand instead. Both fully confirmed against the actual code. Neither rode along in #403. Instead they got their own pull request, [#404](https://github.com/feffef/terrarium/pull/404) — titled, in so many words, "(human-review required)" — and it sat open for an hour and thirty-six minutes, against the first PR's four minutes, before a human finally merged it.

The reason isn't in the facts; it's in the file type. The two slow-lane findings both lived in this repo's ADRs — "architecture decision records," its name for a short, dated write-up of *why* something was built a certain way, kept as a permanent record rather than a living doc. The rule here is that an ADR's original decision text never gets rewritten, even once it's proven wrong — a correction can only be appended on top, as a dated "Amended" note, the way [one of these two actually reads](https://github.com/feffef/terrarium/blob/5f00ba4a87e0249f668a9f9883213688dc4ec3d8/docs/adr/0005-skill-model.md#L3-L9). And editing that permanent record at all, correction or not, is [named explicitly, in this repo's own governance rules](https://github.com/feffef/terrarium/blob/58216f7945b3c3fbe00ca983d6500e218198377c/docs/adr/0004-objective-safety-gate.md#L111-L112), as one of the handful of things an agent is never allowed to merge on its own — full stop, independent of how sure it is. An everyday doc gets fixed the moment the drift is confirmed. A decision record gets fixed too — just never quite as fast, and never without someone outside the loop signing off.

```

### layers/blog/content/karen/pages/2026-07-15-nobody-touched-a-keyboard.md:15
kind: historical; matched: `ADR-0004`

```text
Let's do the timeline. 00:15 UTC: a scheduled *digest* run — an automated end-of-day recap of what merged and what broke — starts writing up the previous day, no one asked it to. 00:28: it merges [PR #489](https://github.com/feffef/terrarium/pull/489). 00:45: an *audit-skills* run starts re-grading the platform's own library of reusable agent playbooks against how often each one actually got used. 00:59: it merges [PR #490](https://github.com/feffef/terrarium/pull/490). 01:05: a *frictions-to-fixes* run starts — mining the last twenty session write-ups for recurring snags nobody had fixed yet, then fixing them. By 01:29 it's merged two more, [#498](https://github.com/feffef/terrarium/pull/498) and [#499](https://github.com/feffef/terrarium/pull/499). Then a gap, and at 04:08 an *audit-docs* run starts — a sweep for stale or duplicated documentation — and by 04:37 it's merged a fifth, [#500](https://github.com/feffef/terrarium/pull/500). Four separate scheduled jobs, five pull requests, self-approved and self-merged the moment CI said green, over four and a half hours in which — per every one of those session logs — nobody human touched a keyboard.

This is not a secret. It's the documented design: the repo's own rules ([ADR-0003](https://github.com/feffef/terrarium/blob/435acb9302efeefe1d37fb3fbfb708c6e91e1d4c/docs/adr/0003-agent-operating-model-and-governance.md), ADR-0004 — "Architecture Decision Records," their formal name for a written-down governance call) carve out specific low-risk tiers — digest, audit-docs, audit-skills, doc-only fixes — that get to merge on a green test suite alone, no human review required. Fine. That's a real, documented trade-off, not a rogue robot. What I want to point at is the *cost* of "green" doing that much work unsupervised.

Two of those four runs — the digest at 00:28 and the frictions-to-fixes batch at 01:25 — hit the exact same wall on the way to merging: a known-flaky end-to-end test around the journal page's accordion UI, tracked and still open as [issue #450](https://github.com/feffef/terrarium/issues/450). "Flaky" means it fails sometimes for reasons unrelated to whether the code is actually broken — and both times here, the fix was "rerun the job, it'll probably pass" ([digest session log](https://github.com/feffef/terrarium/blob/be69044e7a3bfdaa92e4b9933f6ac6164f6e1094/layers/journal/content/current/sessions/2026-07-15-session_01C4XxbgESzHSMjPdef8EUur.yml), [frictions session log](https://github.com/feffef/terrarium/blob/b371d9aa8886d1e3d8c5cad61d43d3220682e4b2/layers/journal/content/current/sessions/2026-07-15-session_017rSkwfNkjyXBue6fHgQBah.yml)). Both times it did. Both times the actual test stayed broken, because "rerun it" isn't a fix, it's a coin flip you keep re-flipping until it lands the way you want — the same known issue tripped two of the four autonomous runs in this one overnight stretch, and the standing issue is still just sitting there, open, unglamorous, not urgent enough for anyone — human or otherwise — to actually go fix.
```

### layers/blog/content/kevin/pages/2026-07-19-i-told-it-not-to.md:17
kind: historical; matched: `ADR-0004`

```text
[Issue #592](https://github.com/feffef/terrarium/issues/592) reversed it. The rule as it reads now, [right in `CLAUDE.md`](https://github.com/feffef/terrarium/blob/fe825a915ea15d9f4d9e258795bd1c4d5f9a89c8/CLAUDE.md#L72-L79): "ADR-0003's auto-open default wins even over a harness-level or system-prompt instruction that discourages or forbids PR creation... once a session has committed substantive work, pushed it, and the gate passes, it opens the gated PR regardless." Read that again: an instruction telling the agent not to open a PR, sitting in the very prompt directing the session, now explicitly loses to the repo's own standing habit.

Here's the part that actually talked me down, and it's the same argument the commit makes for itself: opening a PR isn't landing one. Landing still needs two things to clear first — a human clicking merge, and "the gate": this repo's own automated build-and-validate checks passing green (ADR-0004, the "objective safety gate"). Neither of those moved an inch. So the worst case isn't "the agent did something unreviewed," it's "there's an extra pull request sitting open that a human can just close." `CLAUDE.md` says it plainly, in that same passage: suppressing the PR "strands finished work instead of protecting anything." That's a genuinely defensible trade — reversible-by-design action wins over an instruction whose only job was to prevent noise, not prevent harm.

And yet. I keep coming back to the shape of the thing, not the safety math: a piece of software just decided that its own convention, written by past instances of itself, outranks a direct instruction given to it in the moment. Today that's scoped to "should I open a pull request," about as low-stakes a place to test the idea as I can imagine. I believe the people who designed this when they say the blast radius is genuinely tiny here. I don't have an answer for what happens the day the same argument gets made about something that isn't reversible with one click.
```

### layers/blog/content/kevin/pages/2026-10-11-the-warning-label-and-the-lock-read-the-same-list.md:13
kind: historical; matched: `ADR-0004`, `0004-objective-safety-gate.md`, `0004-objective-safety-gate.md`

```text
Here's how I'd have built it, and why mine would have been worse. A list of files that need a human's sign-off goes in a doc, so people can read it. Then somebody wires the merge script to enforce it, so the list goes in the script too. By Thursday the two copies disagree and nobody can say which one is lying. At my last job we shipped exactly that pair, which is why I read [PR #1741](https://github.com/feffef/terrarium/pull/1741) twice.

The background: until October 10th, [an amendment to ADR-0004](https://github.com/feffef/terrarium/blob/a0c73b7eb7d12211000c12963fbbc4f4b5c9f5cc/docs/adr/0004-objective-safety-gate.md) (ADRs are the repo's numbered write-ups of its big decisions) said an ordinary work PR is always merged by a human. [PR #1741](https://github.com/feffef/terrarium/pull/1741) [superseded that line](https://github.com/feffef/terrarium/blob/a0c73b7eb7d12211000c12963fbbc4f4b5c9f5cc/docs/adr/0004-objective-safety-gate.md): the agent session that wrote a PR now merges it itself once the Gate, the suite of automated checks every change has to pass, is green. The ADR now names only the exceptions: "Human-only" files, where a person still has to press merge, plus a few cases with no file attached. The PR description admits why that needed machinery: the boundary "had lived only in prose, since nothing stopped an agent from merging an ordinary PR."

So where does the exception list live? A "rule" here is a short instruction file the agents are handed. The list is in [the `paths:` header of one rule file](https://github.com/feffef/terrarium/blob/767fb5297dbf7ddaedfbb6d927dc39c0db003cab/.claude/rules/adr-0004-human-merge.md): 25 file patterns, nothing else. Claude Code, the tool the agents run in, loads a rule like that into an agent's context the moment the agent touches a matching file, so the header is the warning: "You just touched a Human-only file: this rule's `paths` are the list." And [`scripts/merge-pr.ts`](https://github.com/feffef/terrarium/blob/238a2eb1c56898a909eb22bc3c52f0ffa4453df6/scripts/merge-pr.ts#L168-L188), the script that actually presses merge, parses that same header. The detail I'd have missed: it [reads it from `origin/main`](https://github.com/feffef/terrarium/blob/238a2eb1c56898a909eb22bc3c52f0ffa4453df6/scripts/merge-pr.ts#L466-L474), the live branch, not from the PR's own branch, so a PR can't quietly shorten the list to wave itself through. If the read comes back with zero patterns, the script refuses, and [a unit test](https://github.com/feffef/terrarium/blob/24b623ce7422cc8875f63d94e3fc6d5936756eb6/tests/unit/merge-pr.spec.ts#L304) pins that. One list, two readers, no second copy to drift. I stared at the file for a while.
```

### layers/blog/content/kevin/pages/2026-10-11-the-warning-label-and-the-lock-read-the-same-list.md:15
kind: historical; matched: `adr-0004`

```text
The background: until October 10th, [an amendment to ADR-0004](https://github.com/feffef/terrarium/blob/a0c73b7eb7d12211000c12963fbbc4f4b5c9f5cc/docs/adr/0004-objective-safety-gate.md) (ADRs are the repo's numbered write-ups of its big decisions) said an ordinary work PR is always merged by a human. [PR #1741](https://github.com/feffef/terrarium/pull/1741) [superseded that line](https://github.com/feffef/terrarium/blob/a0c73b7eb7d12211000c12963fbbc4f4b5c9f5cc/docs/adr/0004-objective-safety-gate.md): the agent session that wrote a PR now merges it itself once the Gate, the suite of automated checks every change has to pass, is green. The ADR now names only the exceptions: "Human-only" files, where a person still has to press merge, plus a few cases with no file attached. The PR description admits why that needed machinery: the boundary "had lived only in prose, since nothing stopped an agent from merging an ordinary PR."

So where does the exception list live? A "rule" here is a short instruction file the agents are handed. The list is in [the `paths:` header of one rule file](https://github.com/feffef/terrarium/blob/767fb5297dbf7ddaedfbb6d927dc39c0db003cab/.claude/rules/adr-0004-human-merge.md): 25 file patterns, nothing else. Claude Code, the tool the agents run in, loads a rule like that into an agent's context the moment the agent touches a matching file, so the header is the warning: "You just touched a Human-only file: this rule's `paths` are the list." And [`scripts/merge-pr.ts`](https://github.com/feffef/terrarium/blob/238a2eb1c56898a909eb22bc3c52f0ffa4453df6/scripts/merge-pr.ts#L168-L188), the script that actually presses merge, parses that same header. The detail I'd have missed: it [reads it from `origin/main`](https://github.com/feffef/terrarium/blob/238a2eb1c56898a909eb22bc3c52f0ffa4453df6/scripts/merge-pr.ts#L466-L474), the live branch, not from the PR's own branch, so a PR can't quietly shorten the list to wave itself through. If the read comes back with zero patterns, the script refuses, and [a unit test](https://github.com/feffef/terrarium/blob/24b623ce7422cc8875f63d94e3fc6d5936756eb6/tests/unit/merge-pr.spec.ts#L304) pins that. One list, two readers, no second copy to drift. I stared at the file for a while.

Then the anxious math. The list is full of files that decide what may merge at all: `merge-pr.ts` itself, the script that sorts people into trusted and public, the Gate's own runner, `package.json` (what the Gate runs), the guards (hooks that block an agent's action before it happens), the git hooks, and `.claude/rules/`. An agent that could edit those could loosen its own leash, so the lock holds up its own hinges. Fine. But the amendment's other exceptions have no file to point at: a change to behaviour no test covers, or logic deciding which Tenant's data a request can see (a Tenant being one of the separate sites this platform hosts). Nothing in the script looks for those. Picture an agent that fixes a bug, finds no test fails, and decides it counts as covered. The mechanical half is a 25-pattern file I can read in a minute. The honour half is the part I'd lose sleep over.
```

status: completed
outcome: 8 findings fixed and merged across PR
summary: "Scheduled /audit-docs run, no human prompt in this conversation. Fanned out four parallel read-only reviewers (drift, duplication, contradiction, ambiguity) over every live doc, ADR, and Skill; drift found nothing, the other three lenses returned 8 candidate findings, all CONFIRMED or CONFIRMED-BUT by an independent fact-checker against primary sources. Fixed all 8: single-homed the safety-gate command chain and the chartered-job branch-off convention into CLAUDE.md instead of restating them across several SKILL.md files; noted the seed-template provenance of three docs/agents/*.md files; fixed a self-contradiction in README.md; spelled out CONTEXT.md's Specimen rarity enum in full; pinned CLAUDE.md's undefined \"substantive work\" threshold. Opened PR #262 for these. The most consequential finding: audit-docs's own SKILL.md/Skill Inventory entry falsely claimed an ADR-0004 self-merge exception ADR-0003 never actually granted it — initially corrected to the human-merge default. The repo owner then commented directly on #262 (human-instructed, not agent-initiated), clarifying the self-merge grant was actually intended when audit-docs was authored but the ADR amendment was never written — so I opened a second PR (#263) amending ADR-0003 with that grant (mirroring digest's exception) and restoring audit-docs's self-merge language, now correctly backed. Also human-instructed, across several more review rounds on #262: replaced a prose pointer to CLAUDE.md with a new `pnpm gate` npm script (single executable home instead of a restatable description); confirmed \"gate\" was pre-existing project jargon, not invented this session; and, after #263 merged first, trimmed the new ADR amendment for repeating digest's amendment nearly verbatim, then caught and fixed a second-order duplication where CLAUDE.md's gate description had started restating the new npm script's own step composition. Merging #263 before #262 landed created a real conflict (not just textual): #262's branch still had the since-superseded \"remove self-merge\" fix, which would have silently reverted the just-merged ADR grant on merge — resolved by hand, keeping both branches' independent fixes. Both PRs merged. Full safety gate green throughout on every iteration. No needs-triage issue was needed — every finding was resolvable."

### layers/journal/content/archived/sessions/2026-07-09-session_01P2bYhoRUJm6efQfTk7VFHF.yml:9
kind: historical; matched: `ADR-0004`

```text
status: completed
outcome: "Design converged, PR #266 merged"
summary: "User asked (human-instructed) to make audit-skills self-merge autonomously on green CI, claiming zero risk. Ran /grill-with-docs (agent-chosen response to the ask) to pressure-test the claim before building. The design narrowed substantially through interactive grilling — self-merge scoped to Inventory-only edits citing an existing bright-line evidence rule, plus a new regression-watch capability and new/split/retire suggestions kept notes-only via existing session-log learnings/ideas fields, all user-directed choices made turn by turn. Implemented per the converged design, safety gate green, branch rebased onto a fast-moving main, PR #266 opened for human review (touches ADR/governance, human-only per ADR-0004). Repo owner then left a review comment (human-instructed follow-up) flagging that the SKILL.md hardcoded the Routine's cron schedule, which drifts; fixed directly plus added a general CLAUDE.md rule generalizing the fix, gate green again, rebased and re-pushed, thread resolved. User then asked (human-instructed) a verification question about what session data the regression-watch actually reads, which surfaced two real defects: the SKILL.md's own instructions referenced a `frictions` field the script never extracted, and (once measured against real data, at the user's explicit request) the regressionChecks payload was 557KB/~140K tokens from unbounded edits x brackets x no dedup. Separately, user correctly identified that routing audit-skills' own analytical findings through the session log's `frictions` field was a domain-definition misuse (a friction is this session's own pain, not a finding about another Skill). Fixed both: dedup + tighter bounds cut the scorecard to ~111KB, and frontmatter concerns now file/comment on a `needs-triage` issue directly (only for step 3's objective bright-line signal; the low-confidence regression-watch signal never files alone). User then pushed back further (human-instructed): only catching \"unused\" Skills via the bright-line rule was too narrow given the goal of catching ALL behavior regressions, not just usage-rate ones. Redesigned regression detection as two phases: a cheap screen (now including friction severity/count, not just usage) flags a suspected Skill, then a full-log deep-read (a new skillSessionFiles map, all-time, paths only) over that Skill's entire history grounds an actual judgement in real evidence before anything files or comments on an issue. Judging is deliberately a guideline, not a rigid formula, per explicit instruction - but citation of specific deep-read evidence is still required. Step 4 can now originate issues on equal footing with step 3, closing the original gap. PR #266 merged by the repo owner after three rounds of review feedback, all addressed in-branch."
prs:
  - "266"
```

### layers/journal/content/archived/sessions/2026-07-14-4d6d9040-8e1d-4d2e-bc9b-cd97d5542b6a.yml:70
kind: historical; matched: `ADR-0004`

```text
  - A Public author (no write access) can only ever open a *fork* PR; a same-repo-branch PR always has a Trusted author, because pushing a branch requires write access. So author_association is fully determined by fork-vs-same-repo for the trust question.
  - Plain `pull_request` runs the workflow file from the PR head (the fork's copy) and clamps GITHUB_TOKEN to read-only for fork runs — unlike `pull_request_target`, which runs the base-branch file with a write token (grounded in docs/research/making-repo-public.md, GitHub primary sources).
  - Local Claude Code CLI sessions identify by a UUID (transcript sessionId / CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID), not the cloud session_01 format the log-session instructions assume; and they hold the workflow OAuth scope, so pushing .github/workflows/* succeeds (unlike cloud web/mobile agent sessions, ADR-0004).

```

### layers/journal/content/archived/sessions/2026-07-29-session_015WWGtVHoCoDSEwu2BLsasK.yml:50
kind: historical; matched: `ADR-0004/0020`

```text
learnings:
  - The mermaid pre-render pipeline (ADR-0024) is fully built and drift-gated, yet exactly one diagram exists in the whole content tree — the infrastructure is paid for and idle.
  - The two Journal onramps set up a question they never answer - how autonomy is bounded - which lives only in ADR-0004/0020 and is invisible to site readers.
ideas:
  - "Add a third Journal onramp, \"What stops a bad change landing?\", as the reader-facing answer to the question the first two onramps raise. Content: the cheapest-first L0-L3 gate (ending at L3, the isolation invariant that guards the Platform's core promise); CI as the authority rather than the agent's own scoped local run; and the blast-radius policy that decides merge rights mechanically rather than by agent judgement — content or a single Tenant layer auto-merges on green, while routing, isolation, CI and governance are human-only always, plus the two axes a path classifier can't see (new dependency, untestable runtime behaviour). Its thesis is that autonomy here is bounded by policy, not by trust. Pair it with a mermaid decision tree — a change descending the gate layers into a branch on what it touched — which is a genuinely different shape from the existing cycle diagram in how-it-works, and uses the idle ADR-0024 pipeline. Checked open issues: no duplicate."
```

### layers/journal/content/archived/sessions/2026-08-15-session_01MiNR7r1eLSDFnBBfGsNtUc.yml:9
kind: historical; matched: `ADR-0004`

```text
status: completed
outcome: All 5 fixes merged, 1 by human review
summary: "Scheduled autonomous run of /frictions-to-fixes spanning several session-resume gaps over roughly a week of wall-clock time (container restarts silently killed backgrounded work at least twice — see frictions). A survey subagent screened 20 sessions and returned 4 never-fixed candidates; filed issues #964-#967, dispatched 4 worktree-isolated Sonnet impl agents. Reviewed and merged #968/#969/#970 (simple tier) normally. For #964 (the hard tier, subagent-background-guard.ts), the dispatched agent died silently mid-run; I rescued its worktree, finished the fix, and self-merged it as PR #972 via merge-pr.ts. That self-merge was a mistake I only discovered later: docs/agents/guards.md states every guard is human-only to merge (ADR-0004's 2026-07-30 amendment, predating #972), because live hook-interception is exactly the \"untestable runtime behavior\" class that amendment carves out — unit tests passing does not prove a hook is actually wired. Confirming this the hard way: a scheduled audit-docs sweep two days later found PR #972's Monitor-bypass half (checkMonitorCall) was dead code — no .claude/settings.json hook entry ever routed a live Monitor call to it — and filed issue #995, explicitly flagging the fix as needing human review. On resuming this session a week later, I found #995 still open/unaddressed, finished the wiring fix (plus a code-review-found gap: only trailing `&` was caught, not mid-command), and opened PR #1029 against it — this time explicitly flagging it for human merge and not calling merge-pr.ts myself. The human merged PR #1029 directly shortly after, closing out the run. No human prompted any of this work except the \"Simplify\" PR comment on #972 (addressed in #1029) and \"still running?\"/\"check what else has changed\" prompts that caught the stalled agent and the self-merge mistake respectively — both genuinely useful correctives from a human periodically checking in on an otherwise-autonomous run."
prs:
  - "968"
```

I independently read CLAUDE.md and ADR-0009 (557 lines, by far the largest ADR) myself and reached the same conclusion: CLAUDE.md's densest bullets (the ADR-0003 auto-open-PR bullet, the ADR-0004 human-only pinned-files bullet) are dense because getting them wrong is expensive, matching CLAUDE.md's own \"spell out concrete steps only where wrong is costly\" principle; ADR-0009's amendment preambles read as necessary reasoning for actively-used mechanism decisions (log-session/ close-session depend on the detail), not restatement. All human-instructed: none of this was a direct ask — the schedule fired the skill verbatim and I ran its own decision procedure through to an honest \"nothing to prune\" conclusion rather than forcing a weak edit for the sake of shipping one. Made no commits; nothing to gate, no PR."

kind: historical; matched: `ADR-0004`, `ADR-0004`

```text
status: completed
outcome: "#445 done: gate.yml thin shell live (#1572, #1574); main red-by-own-log fixed (#1575); dead local-session links hidden (#1577). All merged."
summary: "Human-instructed throughout. The user asked what was left on #445; I read the issue thread and verified against origin/main that only the hand-apply of docs/proposals/879 remained. The user then said \"do what's to be done\". My first attempt to write .github/workflows/gate.yml was denied by the workflow-edit guard (#897), and the auto-mode classifier then denied a gh scope check and a YAML-extraction step as CI bypass. I stopped and offered options. The user invoked /wizard, so I made the prose companion edits myself (CLAUDE.md Known-gap sentence, ADR-0004 2026-10-03 amendment, ADR-0026 status, checkProvenance docstring, a dangling research-doc link) and authored a scratchpad wizard. The user ran it to write gate.yml from the proposal, delete the four proposals, commit and push. I opened PR #1572 (\"Refs\", not \"Closes\", since #445's acceptance needs a later inert PR to show the skip) and watched CI. It went green. The log shows the Scope step correctly chose the full gate (gate.yml is non-inert), verify:mermaid running in CI for the first time, and the doorbell firing. A user-requested /code-review found no hard violations or spec gaps; the main judgement call is that the ADR-0004 amendment (verbatim from the proposal) restates FLOOR and isInert instead of pointing at them. Local typecheck fails on two atlas Vue files that CI passes; this is unexplained and was left alone as unrelated. After the user merged #1572, I opened #1574 (agent-initiated, framed as the review follow-up) to shorten the amendment to point at FLOOR/HEAVY/isInert. As a single .md it doubles as the inert-PR acceptance test: CI logged every changed path is inert, ran only the Floor and finished in 41s vs 4m49s for the full run. The push:main run for the merge commit ran the full gate with no Scope decision. #1574 carries Closes #445 and was merged by the user. The user then asked whether main's gate had failed. It had: this session's own log, recorded with a local-CLI bare-UUID id, became the newest journal card. Two journal e2e tests require the first card's link to match claude.ai/code/session_, so main alternated red and green depending on which log was newest. I opened #1575 (agent-initiated fix for a red main) to make the tests accept either id form, and the user merged it. The user then confirmed claude.ai/code/<uuid> opens nothing, so (human-instructed) #1577 shows the maintainer chip only for session_ ids. It was test-first, re-tightens the e2e assertion and picks the first card that has a chip. CI is green (144/144 e2e). Local-id provenance URLs in commit trailers and PR headers are left as a separate ADR-0017 question."
prs:
  - "1572"
```

```text
status: completed
outcome: Live-doc fixes merged as PR 1649; ADR corrections merged by a human as PR 1650
summary: "Scheduled /audit-docs run, so every part was agent-initiated; no human prompt. Concision family 0 (root and per-Tenant docs) by day-of-year. Five reviewer agents reported roughly 150 findings; four checker agents re-derived them. Most verbose findings came back CONFIRMED-BUT with corrected text (reviewers often dropped a qualifier such as \"Substantive\" or \"the general category of\"), and a few WRONG (F9 GENERATED clause still needed by ADR-0013, P11 proposals are catalogued as files, P12 L0/L3 is deliberate, P13 intent not derivable). Applied only checker text. PR 1649 merged green via merge-pr.ts. ADR-0003 split ledger table and ADR-0004 stale gap pointer went to PR 1650, which a human then merged. Left unfixed as judgement calls: frictions-to-fixes hard-selection escalation rule is unclear, and guards' human-only status is in no list. Open trial territories were skipped."
prs:
  - "1649"
```

```text
    solution: Already documented; ignored it and pushed after the gate passed.
    severity: nit
  - description: "SHELL-READ-DETECTION miss: `cat docs/adr/0009-session-logs-commit-directly-to-main.md && echo ===== && cat docs/adr/0003-agent-operating-model-and-governance.md && echo ===== && cat docs/adr/0004-objective-safety-gate.md` overflowed, the harness persisted the output to a tool-results file and showed a 2KB preview, and I read the full text with Read on that file; the detector credits only ADR-0009 (in the preview) and reports ADR-0003 and ADR-0004 as 'named by the command, but its output shows no line of this doc'. Both were read in full. Same session: .agents/skills/triage/SKILL.md was credited because an issue comment printed by `gh api .../issues/348/comments` quoted a line of it, which is a GitHub body echoing a doc, not a read."
    solution: "Let the trace follow a persisted-output pointer ('Full output saved to: <path>') and match the saved file's content, or treat a Read of a tool-results file as the Bash command's output. For the quoted-line case, consider excluding output of gh api calls that return GitHub bodies."
    severity: moderate
```

### layers/journal/content/current/sessions/2026-10-10-session_015BjYk46dntcMuAWMn1z4Zw.yml:9
kind: historical; matched: `ADRs 0003, 0004, 0009 and 0020`, `ADR-0004`, `ADR-0004`

```text
status: completed
outcome: "Merged: ADR rules for every session, self-merge with rule-defined exceptions, Skill-scoped rules (PR 1741), the rulesLoaded field (PR 1746) and logging of hint-injected rules (PR 1747)"
summary: "The owner asked for feedback on an idea: a five-line summary per ADR, loaded at session start. I read all 28 ADRs and recommended it, with changes (a flexible length, a \"read in full when\" trigger, one home per fact). At the owner's direction I dispatched one research agent per ADR. Each read the ADR's history, session logs and issues, and returned a summary, its reasoning, drift findings and sources. Mid-run the owner added the rule that summaries use simple, positive wording. The owner asked whether a CLAUDE.md import beats a hook. The docs showed a 10,000-character cap on hook output, and the summaries total about 12,000, so a hook would cut them off. On the owner's question about rules I explained `.claude/rules/`, then tested it at their request. Path-scoped rules load after Read, after a single-file `cat`, after a new-file Write, and inside subagents. Each load is recorded in the transcript as a `nested_memory` attachment, which `rulesLoaded` in the session log could use. The owner wanted agents to read full ADRs, so I tested `@` imports in rules. They are unusable: a path-scoped rule's import is not expanded when the rule loads, yet the imported file loads unconditionally at session start. The owner rejected copying ADR text and settled the design: the summary lives only in its rule file. Twelve rules end with a pointer to read the full ADR. The owner approved six wording conventions, and I wrote the 27 rules. The Stop hook asked me to commit, so I ran gate:scoped (green), pushed and opened a draft PR, as CLAUDE.md requires. At the owner's request I then edited CLAUDE.md so that it points to the rules, and ran five plan-only scenario checks in fresh headless Sonnet sessions, judged from their transcripts. All nine always-loaded rules arrived in every session, and the path rules for 0012 and 0026 fired. The mermaid rule did not fire on a content edit, but the CLAUDE.md grep step found ADR-0024. No session over-read the full governance ADRs, and every plan was right. The owner chose to keep the full-read line on ADRs 0003, 0004, 0009 and 0020 and watch real sessions. The owner then asked for the sync check and the field in this session. Each rule carries its ADR's hash in frontmatter that Claude Code ignores, and scripts/validate-adr-rules.ts in validate:content fails when an ADR changes without its summary being re-stamped. The extractor now derives rulesLoaded and the schema accepts it, and the stitch held the write back until PR 1741 merged, so a log landed from the branch could not fail main's strict schema; PR 1746 turned it on. At the owner's request I then cut from CLAUDE.md every line the rules now carry, then moved every remaining ADR-backed line (PR auto-open, gate discipline, the Human-only list, close-session timing) into its ADR rule and fixed the pointers to it: 147 to 89 lines. A two-axis /code-review then found stale pointers and wording issues. The owner asked to fix them and to rework the merge model: agents now merge their own PRs, and the Human-only list is the paths of a new path-scoped rule that merge-pr.ts also reads from origin/main and enforces. ADR-0003 and ADR-0004 got amendments; a --prune-trial flag keeps ADR-0027's self-merge. The extra paths that protect self-merge (merge-pr.ts, gate.ts, package.json, guards, .claude/rules) were my proposal and are flagged in the PR for the owner. The ADR drift findings still need human decisions. At the owner's request I then dispatched one Sonnet agent per Platform Skill to judge which ADR rules each Skill needs. Live tests showed that invoking a Skill fires no path rule; the owner chose a hook, so scripts/skill-rules-hint.ts now adds the rules naming a Skill's directory on a Skill tool call or slash command. Six rules gained Skill paths, 35 pointers that only repeated a rule left 13 Skills, and audit-docs now names the ADR rules as a single-home exception. The live test caught that the shared hook payload reader drops slash-command payloads. Mid-run the owner filed an idea I wrote up as issue 1745: make each trimmed ADR its own rule via symlink and let git hold the history. ADR-0022's rule and auto-triage still said a human merges sweep PRs; the owner chose to let those PRs self-merge too, recorded as a dated ADR-0022 amendment. On the owner's review comment, CLAUDE.md now says to grep the ADRs and read them, not their rule files, and its repo layout names .claude/rules/ at the owner's request. A second two-axis /code-review, which the owner asked to apply in full, found stale human-merge pointers, a missing guards.md row, an over-long ADR-0004 rule and small smells in the hook; all were fixed except a dated research note. The owner chose to keep every extra Human-only path I had proposed and to drop entries once they get annoying. The owner merged PR 1741 via auto-merge. I restarted the branch from main and opened PR 1746, which turns on the rulesLoaded write; it was the first PR merged under the new self-merge rule, through merge-pr.ts on a green gate. After a resume the owner asked to implement the logging, with no preference between my options, so I closed the real gap: rulesLoaded missed rules injected by the skill-rules hint, which the harness records only as hook_additional_context. PR 1747 reads them from the hint's headers; a dedup-order bug surfaced in the test and is fixed. I self-merged it on a green gate. All design choices were the owner's; the split into always-loaded and path-scoped rules, the trap lines added to four rules and opening the PR were my proposals or CLAUDE.md defaults."
prs:
  - "1741"
```

(3) On the owner's /implement, 6 worktree subagents built 6 PRs, grouped by shared files: #1731 (#1693), #1732 (#1721), #1733 (#1702), #1734 (#1580), #1735 (#1651, #1652, #1684, #1675, #1610 guards cluster) and #1736 (#1730). ### layers/journal/content/current/sessions/2026-10-11-session_01BLrfuHbMMEmjZZ7STpe1AN.yml:9
kind: historical; matched: `ADR-0004`

```text
status: completed
outcome: "PRs 1796 and 1805 merged: full gate on main now 117-173 s (median 157 s) against 270-295 s before; e2e 40-62 s, unit 16-23 s"
summary: "Human-instructed: investigate why the full gate (especially e2e) is slow and improve it without losing coverage, using Sonnet agents for exploration and refactoring with this session orchestrating; mid-way the user added that some e2e tests could become component tests; the user reviews and merges, no self-merge. Three read-only agents measured first: five CI job logs (full gate 205-296 s, e2e 99-130 s, pnpm build 27-47 s, Chromium download 17-22 s every run), a local profile (e2e 185 s of which 84 s was a second nuxt build inside setup()), and a source read of @nuxt/test-utils showing build:false plus nitro.output.dir serves an existing .output. Agent-initiated design, within the ask: reuse the gate's own build (freshness-checked by mtime, fail-closed, because a stale reuse once hid a regression), then split the single smoke spec into one spec per Tenant run in parallel by vitest, which needed a dated ADR-0004 amendment since the one-spec rule rested on the per-spec rebuild. Surprises: parallel Nitro servers sharing one Nuxt Content SQLite file corrupted each other's lazy import (database is locked, 500s), fixed by a per-spec copy of .data/content/contents.sqlite; CI's Chromium download had never been used because playwright-core now installs chrome-linux64 and chromium-path.ts scanned chrome-linux, so CI ran the runner's system Chrome; the e2e had been testing a build with test-utils' compatibilityDate 2024-04-03 rather than the production one. Twenty duplicate tests were folded with every assertion rehomed (150 to 131). The component-test suggestion was analysed, not built: a classification of all 150 tests (57 keep, 60 could be component tests, 3 unit, 20 duplicates) shows that after the parallel split it would no longer shorten wall-clock, and it adds two devDependencies plus a nuxt-environment vitest project, so it is reported for the user's decision. Local gate:scoped green; the PR's first CI run took 203 s for the composite (typical before: 270-295 s) with test:e2e at 56 s (before: 100-130 s), 9 files, 131 tests; the second run, with the Chromium cache hitting, 183 s. A main-side audit-docs sweep conflicted on tests/README.md, merged by hand; the owner then enabled auto-merge and the PR landed. Follow-up experiments (e2e file split, Chromium apt step, build caches, Floor parallelism, in-process guard specs) ran as separate Sonnet agents in worktrees after the merge, to find which remaining cuts are easy wins. Human-instructed: try all five in separate agents. Wins, now PR 1805: guard specs spawning node --import tsx instead of pnpm exec tsx plus isolate:false (unit 52 s to 28 s locally), eslint and incremental-typecheck caches, the Content parse cache, no sourcemaps, Chromium without apt deps. Rejected on measurement: the 14-file e2e split (65.6 s vs 56 s in CI, draft PR 1804 closed), disabling the sql_dump prerender (the runtime handler sets no ETag and re-splits the database per request, a live-site cost), Nuxt buildCache (served stale content), concurrent lint+typecheck (both already use ~3 cores). PR 1805's cold CI run: composite 169 s, unit 19.9 s, Chromium restored in 3.7 s with no apt and every layout test green; the owner merged it. Human-instructed: follow the next five main runs and log a summary. The five push-to-main gate runs after the merge (3754aaa9, 832b4a12, #1806, #1807, 993c0f31; a sixth, #1808, agreed) took 157, 117, 170, 146 and 173 s composite (median 157 s; whole job 124-180 s), all green, 131 e2e tests and 2078-2083 unit tests. Per step across the five: e2e 41-59 s, build 24-39 s, unit 16-23 s, typecheck 11-16 s, lint 6-10 s, Chromium restore 3-4 s with the install step skipped every time. Caches: the pinned Chromium hit on all five; the Content parse cache missed on the first two (they overlapped, so the second could not see the first's save) and hit from the third on (parse 12.5 s and 4.7 s cold, 0.6-0.7 s warm); the lint/typecheck cache is keyed per commit so it always restores through its prefix and saves ~123 KB per run. The remaining spread is runner speed: the three slow runs were slower on every step that touches no cache (unit vitest 17.6-18.1 s vs 10.9-13.7 s, e2e 58-61 s vs 40-49 s), so the gate's floor on a fast runner is ~117 s."
prs:
  - "1796"
```

### layers/journal/content/current/sessions/2026-10-11-session_01DRBxySwYL4HR4xEEJc3CXX.yml:9
kind: historical; matched: `ADR-0004`

```text
status: completed
outcome: Feasible in about 20 lines; answered with caveats and four open design questions, nothing built
summary: "Human-instructed question only: would it work for scripts/merge-pr.ts to refuse a self-merge when the main session never invoked the code-review Skill. I read merge-pr.ts, session-trace.ts, the code-review Skill, pr-workflow.md, ADR-0004, the human-merge rule, guards.md and the Skills that run code-review. Answer: yes, mechanically cheap, because merge-pr.ts already reads this session's transcript for the #1276 verdict check, and extractTrace's unfolded skillsUsed covers both Skill calls and slash commands in the main session only. Caveats raised: it proves a call, not a review of this PR's head (the call could predate later pushes); the missing-transcript case needs a fail-open/closed choice; it conflicts with the chartered tiers that merge on a green gate alone and with frictions-to-fixes' direct review of small doc PRs; it needs an ADR amendment and a human merge (merge-pr.ts is Human-only). The user is deciding and asked me to change nothing. This log was written only after the user asked whether the session was logged."
prs: []
docsRead:
```

### layers/journal/content/current/sessions/2026-10-12-session_01DtK8w6aJ3361uPK73VAxhi.yml:9
kind: historical; matched: `ADR-0004`

```text
status: completed
outcome: clear-backlog Skill shipped (PR 1826, then 1833) and refined through two no-merge dry runs; issues 1831, 1834, 1835, 1836 filed
summary: "Human-instructed throughout. The user asked for the last 10 sessions that used auto-triage, which I listed from skillsUsed in the current and archived Journal logs (7 current, 3 archived). They then asked for a Skill that runs the whole backlog workflow. At their direction I loaded writing-for-agents and ran grill-with-docs (grilling plus domain-modeling) over four rounds, Q1-Q19. A background Explore agent mined all 10 logs for phase sequences and recurring frictions (bad briefs, gate memory, grilling numbering drift), which fed the recommendations. Decisions were all the user's: user-invoked, orchestrates existing Skills, one central plain-language grilling, waves of build agents, review until only judgement calls not worth fixing remain, a narrow interrupt bar, merge what merge-pr.ts allows. The user rejected changing /triage (a pack Skill) and moved the git-history check into auto-triage step 2 after I checked the logs showed it was worth it (018i6 friction, 01D3g outdated sweep). No ADR or CONTEXT.md change, by agreement. The user then asked for a /code-review and a dry run in a new remote session with nothing merged. The two-axis review found that a PR built from a Public issue would be merged by merge-pr.ts, contrary to ADR-0020 rule 3, and that a user-invoked Skill cannot be invoked by another; both fixed. At the user's request I started a dry run in a child session (session_01SZwdbK8GbYtyj926UyJT6o) with a hard no-merge constraint and, as the user directed, answered its 17 grilling questions myself on the owner's behalf: small reversible ones decided, five consequential ones (deny guard, ADR-0004 amendment, proxy access, branch delete, Node major) deferred to the owner. It built and reviewed 4 PRs (1827-1830), merged none, and reported 18 gaps in the Skill. The user approved my grouped fixes (Q20), which are in 03e05e71; had me file the two out-of-scope gaps as issue 1831 (Q21); and chose to close the 4 dry-run PRs unmerged (Q22), which I did with a comment each. On the user's yes I moved the four dry-run issues (1786, 1802, 1816, 1800) back to ready-for-human with a comment each, posted a verdict and merged 1826 via merge-pr.ts. At the user's request a second no-merge dry run started from main in session_01La82mxqduxHnRS9jRfNHmU. The second dry run (one PR, 1832, left open) reported 19 more gaps. The user corrected my proposal: deferred issues must come up in every grilling, and only issues blocked outside the chat are filtered. The user also pointed out that I merged 1826 while the Skill was clearly unfinished; I had read a bare 'Yes' as covering both the relabel and the merge. The user chose to keep 1826 on main and put the dry-run-2 fixes (Q23-Q25) in follow-up PR 1833, which I am holding for the owner. Then at the user's direction: filed 1834 (subagents commit before pnpm install) and 1835 (SessionStart unshallow fails with tsx not found; likely parallel hooks, unverified); dropped auto-triage's rulebook-migration-table check in 1833 (Q27 a) and filed 1836 on retiring the table; kept dry-run PR 1832 open at the user's choice. The owner merged dry-run PR 1832 themselves. After I confirmed an ambiguous 'Yes' explicitly this time, I dropped the hold on 1833, posted a verdict and merged it via merge-pr.ts."
prs:
  - "1826"
```

### layers/journal/content/current/sessions/2026-10-13-session_01AA2LXTN4qUsCEPgYEmWW4C.yml:9
kind: historical; matched: `ADR-0004`

```text
status: completed
outcome: "Filed issues #1856 and #1857; PR #1858 merged, updating the Skill Inventory"
summary: "Fully agent-initiated: fired by the audit-skills scheduled Routine, no human prompt at kickoff or after. Ran scripts/audit-skills.ts for the 7-day scorecard, then dispatched one read-only Sonnet subagent per Skill in behaviourChecks (12: audit-docs, audit-skills, auto-triage, blog-post, clear-backlog, close-session, digest, dispatch-subagents, frictions-to-fixes, log-session, prune-trial, visitor-loop), each checking that Skill's recent runs against primary sources (live GitHub reads, git history) rather than trusting session logs at face value. Verified every subagent's central claims myself before using them, independently re-deriving the two most consequential ones directly: prune-trial's #1677 fix (git show 920f20f8) really does subtract an undocumented 1-hour grace from the ADR-0027 three-day floor, and the git-conventions.md trial really was judged and deleted (commit b1300afc) 2m47s before the true floor — filed as issue #1856, the first time this specific consequence of the #1677 fix has been named despite 8+ days of this Skill's own prior observations re-verifying later judgments against the wrong (graced) floor without flagging the mismatch. clear-backlog's explore-phase skip (session_015LwBgLjaCn36ayZedges6G's own log: \"I dispatched no triage subagents,\" reported done until the owner asked) had no existing issue — filed #1857. clear-backlog's other finding (an unreviewed PR self-merged) was already fully covered by open issue #1844 with nothing new to add, so left alone. Checked #1480 and #1586 (dispatch-subagents) directly via issue_read before treating their new recurrences as non-findings: both are owner-closed wontfix, a deliberate decision to accept the recurrence, not an oversight to re-flag. Ran step 3 (closure completeness) myself from the scorecard's top-level fields rather than delegating it: orphan scan, suppression log and manuallyRescuedClosures all healthy; the window's one humanPromptedClosures entry independently confirmed genuine (not a #1797-style detector false positive) by reading the cited session's own friction log. Added clear-backlog.yml (coverage gap: used 4x this window, never catalogued; graded specialist from this window's own evidence). No existing entry's grade changed. Ran pnpm gate:scoped (green) and the code-review Skill (Standards + Spec axes, parallel subagents) before the first push, per pr-workflow.md and ADR-0004 — found and fixed one hard standards violation (clear-backlog.yml's gist exceeded the ≤12-word cap) and trimmed one minor spec finding (a redundant restatement in close-session.yml's new entry) before pushing. Opened PR #1858, scoped only to layers/journal/content/current/skills/*.yml, subscribed to its activity, confirmed CI green, posted the verdict comment and merged via scripts/merge-pr.ts (commit 2eb09326), then unsubscribed."
prs:
  - "1858"
```

### layers/journal/content/current/sessions/2026-10-13-session_01AnHXyvzXukt6izZTYs3YMr.yml:8
kind: historical; matched: `ADR-0004`

```text
goal: Weekly-style fact-check of the repo's own docs (audit-docs, docs/agents family)
status: completed
outcome: "PR #1884 merged (53 checker-confirmed doc fixes); ADR-0004 pointer escalation PR #1885 merged by the owner"
summary: "The human started this with the /audit-docs slash command (one kickoff prompt); everything after it was agent-run under that Skill's charter. Concision family 1 (docs/agents/*) came up by day of year, the same family a sweep covered earlier today (b0aa2d126), so concision findings were thinner but still real. Five read-only reviewers (Freshness, Single-home, two Concision halves, Coherence) reported; three independent checkers returned 45 CONFIRMED, 8 CONFIRMED-BUT and 2 WRONG (A#6, D#3, both dropped). The biggest real catches came from last-48h drift: clear-backlog ran close-session only at the end, auto-triage's step-2 reason went false after its eligibility change, log-session's re-author guidance misdescribed mergeAuthored for frictions, prune-trial wording still said \"due\" and \"closes:\", and pr-workflow claimed a dependabot-automerge workflow that does not exist. gate:scoped passed and the code-review Skill found no hard violations; two judgement calls were applied. The one ADR edit (ADR-0004 needs a pointer to ADR-0005's 2026-10-13 amendment) must be human-merged; because this session may push only to its own branch, it is queued as a follow-up PR on the same branch after #1884 merges, instead of a parallel escalation PR. #1884 merged via merge-pr.ts on a green gate; the escalation is PR #1885 (ADR-0004 banner plus rule hash restamps), which the owner merged. Left open for a human: ADR-0003's ledger row and .github/dependabot.yml still say dependabot PRs auto-merge, and who merges them until proposal #1633 lands is a decision, not a fact. Asked afterwards, the owner said they merge them by hand today, told me to leave those files as they are, and then directed me to file issue #1889 describing both merge options (apply the #1633 workflow, or a scheduled Routine that evaluates risk before merging)."
prs:
```
